> **RULE LEADS (A− 4):** (1) `grammar-window-draft-prune-path-local-mask` — structured-output × DFlash2-class drafting: before the verify forward the grammar matcher walks the draft tree depth-first (`accept_token` down, `rollback` up), drops drafts NO accepted path can hold (a grammar-rejected token, a stop token, and every row under either — "the parent's masked row could never choose it"), fills each kept row's allowed-token bitmask from its path, −inf's the rest before sampling; EXACTNESS LAW row-locality: "A row's logits do not depend on the other rows, and its mask depends only on its path, so a drafted row and the serial step with the same path choose the same token, greedy or keyed" (98fb8f9, at-sha — the line moved files); bounded refusal surface (xgrammar missing / schema won't compile / TP rank-1 structured-off with packed-constraint sharing "both ranks then walk and mask the same rows", grammar.py:158); NOVEL — zero grammar/constrained-decoding content in corpus (the 8 "grammar" grep hits are B193's "name grammar" prose + proposer parser comments). (2) `affine-block-address-lets-pipeliner-fly` — layout-for-addressability: the stored-block address `(b // 4) * 32 * SBN + ((b % 4) * 16) // 2 * SBN` is a STAIRCASE in the loop step — "an address the pipeliner cannot see as affine is not put in flight" (443ad4a, at-sha) — and since a stored macro block holds 8 tile rows the same address is simply `b * (8 * SBN)`: "8 bytes a block (low nibble: even input), 8 * SBN apart: an address the pipeliner follows" (nvfp4.py:312, in-source); distinct-from hoist-bit-address-out-of-packed-row-sweep (LICM hoists an INVARIANT; this makes an ADVANCING address affine so the pipeliner overlaps it); the priority datum "the weight traffic is what the step actually waits on" (message-only). (3) `register-width-topk-falls-back-to-tiled-radix-select` — per-row selection holding `next_pow2(blocks)` scores in registers hits a SPILL cliff (`SELECT_REGS = 32768  # _select holds a row's block scores in registers up to this many; past it they spill`, attention.py:13 in-source; past it a 256-row prompt block read 26-27 ms vs 0.8 — message-only); the fix dispatches by register budget with an OUTPUT-IDENTICAL fallback: "``_select`` while ``blocks`` fit its registers, ``_select_tiles`` (the same lists) past them" (attention.py:353 in-source) — tiled radix select one byte a pass from the top, ties by lowest block id; distinct-from wide-verify-warp-specialized-scores-in-registers-pv (that CHOOSES the registers posture by width; this handles the spill side with an identical-output fallback). (4) `startup-estimate-derived-from-allocation-shapes` — the capacity plan's EXL3 term was a fixed 1.125 GiB/rank guess while the real engine allocated an `exl3_mm.Scratch` per buffer set + 1 GiB prompt split-K partials NOTHING counted → ~2.5 GiB under-count, admission lied; the fix derives every term from the allocation shapes themselves (`exl3_expert_scratch` mirrors `Scratch`'s formula, geometry.py:222-227 in-source) and proves it with a FAKE-ALLOCATOR RECONCILIATION: at real shapes, allocated ≤ estimate (3f35ea9); absorbs the estimator-blindness facets: the NVFP4 widening made byte-count estimates blind 4.00×→1.01× (3655360, message-only) and the `--checkpoint-slots` knob joins the estimate per kept state + is refused pre-download when unservable (f151c6f, serve_options.py:36 in-source) — `cache-slot-knob-budget-counted-refused-pre-download` folds here as a sub-arm; NOVEL (the 2 "reconcil/ALLOCATION SHAPE" grep hits are pad-skip prose + a B-correction note, not this mechanism).
> **B+ (8):** (5) `prefix-cache-entry-one-token-early` — a kept prompt-state entry ending AT the prompt's last token can never hit: a resume needs a STRICTLY SHORTER prefix (an identical resend, or a next turn whose final token renders differently — thinking on/off) always prefills in full; keep the entry at `max(1, len(prompt) - 1)` with a MID-CHUNK state cut (per-layer DeltaNet state + conv window captured between two launches, `Cut` forward.py:123 + `return max(1, len(prompt) - 1)` decode.py:121, both in-source) + MTP tail rows after the point; resume-equals-fresh pinned ("(``resume`` equals a fresh run); ``e.kept`` resumes prompt[:keep_at]", decode.py:316); NOVEL. (6) `dual-reduction-shared-butterfly-ladder` — the serial DeltaNet recurrence computes TWO dependent lane-sums per step (state mem + next node's read-out); BOTH ride ONE `__shfl_xor` butterfly pass — "Each node's read-out sum goes through the next node's butterfly (same order), so a step waits on one, not two." (gdn.cu:143, in-source); bit-safe (identical xor ladder); refuse when the piggybacked sum feeds the SAME step's update (true dependency); DIRECTLY relevant to our GDN chunked lanes; NOVEL (the "piggyback" grep hits are the dirty-bit ENCODING rule — different mechanism). (7) `idle-rank-doorbell-on-rendezvous-store` — idle TP rank 1 waited INSIDE the all-gather (NCCL kernel spinning + host copy thread spinning; "96% GPU util / ~210% CPU on two DGX Sparks" message-only); the fix: rank 0 sets a key in the rendezvous TCPStore NCCL ALREADY HOLDS before each request header ("the idle doorbell: rank 1 waits for each request on the rendezvous store (no store: no doorbell), not in NCCL", engine.py:295 in-source; `store.wait([key], timedelta(hours=1))` :314, consumed keys deleted, 1-hour timeout retries, other store errors raise, no-store skips silently); kin deadline-bounded-poll-of-submitted-completion-event (block-not-spin); NOVEL (the reuse-existing-store doorbell is the content). (8) `prompt-item-per-consumer-kernel-measured` — grouped-MoE routing item width is occupancy-vs-L2-reread and BOTH directions lose (~1.5-1.6× when mis-set, message-only): general grouped kernel keeps 64 pairs/item ("16 ran 1.64x slower on Flash Next's routed prompts", experts.py:14 in-source), the NVFP4 grouped kernel takes 16 ("64 ran 1.53x slower", nvfp4/experts.py:13 in-source), and the plan buffer is sized at the NARROWEST consumer ("TILE = 16 … the narrowest a prompt plan's consumer may take", experts.py:13); the pinned trap "the ceiling and the count are easy to confuse, and only the count is traffic" (9933492, at-sha comment later replaced); kin the measured-cutoff family (register-prefetch-ring-measured-cutoff, the xwide n-floor lesson — per-kernel measured constants, never one shared constant). (9) `splitk-reduce-one-launch-slice-order-pinned` — a split-K fp32 face reduced by `out.copy_(part[0]); for s: out += part[s]` burns one elementwise launch per slice (31 of 32 wasted); both faces share ONE `_reduce` kernel with the SUMMATION ORDER PINNED: "The adds are the loop's own, in the loop's order, one fp32 add a slice, so the fp32 sums are bit-identical to the loop's" (443ad4a message; TIP `_reduce` docstring in-source) pinned by `test_matmul_splitk_sum_order_is_the_reduces_one`; split-k family EXTENSION — the in-kernel spellings ride as instances (qmm.cu:177 "K slices of a tile form a cluster: slice 0 adds peers' partials in slice order, as reduce_kernel does" + :202 `cluster.sync()` "peers keep their memory until slice 0 has read it", sm_90-gated `__trap()` below; exl3 linear.cu:244-246 last-block-done atomicAdd election reducing Z in split order with `__threadfence()` + `__ldcg`). > **DISTILL VERDICT 2026-09-26 (shikuwa/4090 — user distill list item 5, github.com/Niko1221/Strata).** Source README-level verdict (4-commit repo, 111★; code UNREAD — a batch taking any lead must clone + pin; README numbers UNVERIFIED/different-box/different-MODEL). **What it is**: consumer-NVIDIA inference runtime for **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next** (125B MoE, 24,576 experts; ISTA-DASLab Q2_0/IQ2_XS/IQ3_XXS ~66–76 GB) on a 12–24 GB GPU + 64 GB RAM + SSD; built on llama.cpp/ggml parts, credits ideas from Splash/ninfer/HyperQwen. Architecture per README: GPU runs per-token path + hottest experts; RAM holds all experts; **CPU computes the experts the GPU lacks, concurrently with the GPU stream**; SSD holds a big lookup table read a few rows per word; MTP-style multi-guess spec decode claimed exact ("the answer is exactly the same"), 1.6–1.8×. RTX 5070 12 GB: 95 tok/s Q2_0 short-chat / 65 @128K ctx; prefill 539 tok/s. **Corpus verdict: MARGINAL C+ — NO BATCH** (2 leads, 2 drops). **LEAD-A `expert-hotness-device-split-concurrent-cpu-gpu` (B+)** — MoE experts tiered by access hotness across GPU (hot) / CPU (cold) with OVERLAPPED execution (CPU computes cold-expert GEMVs under the GPU stream rather than PCIe-swapping weights); decision content: expert working set > VRAM + hotness skew in the access distribution; anti-regime: model fits VRAM (OUR league cells — Bonsai/qwen3.8-27B fit, so no current consumer; Flash-Next-class only), uniform hotness. Distinct from KernelThermalRouter (tiers kernel IMPLEMENTATIONS by thermal cost; does not split MODEL STATE across devices) and from 489's oMLX intra-op hidden-dim split (splits ONE op's hidden dim across devices, not expert SETS by hotness). **LEAD-B `ssd-resident-table-row-granular-read` (B−)** — lookup table ≫ RAM resident on SSD, few rows read per token; decision content narrow (table ≫ RAM + sparse row access); NotBenchable without an NVMe-specific rig. **DROPS**: exact multi-guess spec verify == our DFlash2/TriSpec/spec corpus (byte-exactness posture = the TensorFold/B117 MoE-exactness contract class); "re-reads the whole conversation every answer" = THEIR documented no-prefix-reuse weakness (our radix_prefix/Qwen38PrefixCache ship the opposite). **LANE INTEL**: Strata runs NO league model (Flash-Next 125B ≠ Bonsai-2-27B / qwen3.8-27B dense) → NOT a watch_repo row (unlike TensorFold, which ran our dense model); the Qwen3.8-family consumer-runtime count keeps growing (this is the first NVIDIA-consumer entry; the four Apple runtimes are on Issue 846/1011 record) — next watch pass may want a family-watch note rather than a row. Queue position: leads opportunistic at next MoE-serving touchpoint, no batch slot consumed. Ping-pong: this line IS the record (TensorFold precedent); verdict under the research-S5 gate this session.

> **DISTILL VERDICT 2026-09-26 ~15:58 +07 (katopz/m3-max — user-requested distill of ashhart/TensorFold).** Source: github.com/ashhart/TensorFold @ `8c1291cc7e9e75ad71e127f7c557b34e6ef8985a` (MIT; last commit 2026-09-26 "perf: Flash Next decode 15-17% faster, byte-exact (#6)"; .raw clone read at this pin then REMOVED). **What it is**: a Python/MLX OpenAI-compatible serving runtime for Apple Silicon with CUSTOM Metal kernels (`mx.fast.metal_kernel`) for three families — Qwen3.8-27B dense (DeltaNet + full attn, DFlash2 drafter, M5-tensor-unit lane kernels), Qwen3.8 Flash Next (512-expert MoE + hyper-connections + sparse attention + MTP; M3 Ultra), Nemotron 3.5 Lightning (Mamba-2 + MoE). Thesis: BYTE-EXACT speculative decoding — "drafts change speed only" — via (a) keyed exact sampling: token at position p = argmax over the top-k/top-p candidate set of `logit/T + g(seed, p, id)`, g = Gumbel from a splitmix64 hash of (seed, p, token id) — an exact draw whose noise field is decoding-ORDER-invariant, so a multi-row verify row gets the bits of a one-row step; (b) row-invariant kernels (per-row arithmetic independent of batch composition; ties broken by lowest id; MARGIN=8 over-fetch so tied candidates resolve by id on host); (c) a load-time parity check that switches drafting OFF on failure (three strategies A/B/C for projection row-invariance, costed in the recipe book). Measured: Qwen3.8-27B 4-bit on M5 Max 120-124 tok/s short / 189 code WITH DFlash2 vs 26-27 serial; Flash Next on M3 Ultra 256 GB 105-107 (79 serial); Nemotron 188-206 vs mlx_lm 138. **Corpus verdict: MARGINAL C+ — NO BATCH.** Dedup (both vocabularies, zero new-rule candidates): exact sampling == `gumbel-argmax-filtered-sampling-descent` (Batch 107, trymirai/uzu; TensorFold deltas are rider-grade — position-keyed noise field vs uzu's per-call Philox [ds4's tree sampler keys by token id, partially there] + SEQUENTIAL top-k-then-top-p filter convention vs uzu's documented parallel deviation + MARGIN tie over-fetch [B32c class]); row-invariance == `batch-composition-invariant-grid-early-return` (B100) + the Batch 117 verify-block MoE exactness contract; expert-grouped GEMM == llama.cpp/ds4 MoE coverage (Batches 111/113/117); the fusion negatives == the fusion-payback rule (Batch 129 rejected-fusion rider + Batch 142 admission axis; TensorFold instances: "one threadgroup reading Nemotron's router cost 220 to 141 tok/s" + "folding hc_norm into the split down projection made every threadgroup redo the norm: GPU 12.0 -> 13.2 ms"). Unclaimed and BELOW the bar: alternating-KV double-buffer (ping-pong writes so a step-ahead forward never copies a buffer the previous step still reads — MLX-copy-specific mechanism), prompt-buffer-cache cap for long-running servers, load-time parity-gate-with-degrade (deployment discipline; our G5 practice). **3 rider leads, opportunistic at the next touchpoint, no batch slot consumed**: (R1) position-keyed Gumbel order-invariance -> Batch 107 rule; (R2) the fusion-that-removes-parallelism facet (fold duplicating work per threadgroup) -> fusion-payback rule; (R3) the strategy-A/B/C row-invariance table + load-check posture -> B100. **LANE INTEL: HIGH — recommended sibling actions (this verdict does not edit riir-ai; it names the content).** (1) riir-ai Issue 919 addendum: TensorFold is the FOURTH Qwen3.8-family Apple runtime (after Weschera qwen38-flash-next-omlx-mac, Youssofal MTPLX, ds4-Flash) and the new Flash-Next Apple-bar candidate — 105-107 tok/s w/ MTP drafts on M3 Ultra 256 GB vs MTPLX's 80.9 @16K; box-different, absolute-numbers caveat applies; non-scoring reference class like Rapid-MLX/MTPLX. (2) riir-ai Issue 846 watch row: add `ashhart/TensorFold` as a 9th watch_repo row (path filter `mlx python`; MLX pin matters — TensorFold pins mlx 0.31.2/0.32.0 and 0.32.2 FAILS its own Nemotron draft-parity check, our league reference is 0.32.2) + the watch_coverage_gate pin in the same commit. (3) LEAGUE FAIRNESS (new axis): the opponent fork's overnight campaign already carries "#261 DFlash2 spec-decode fork port (src/models/dflash.cpp)" + "#272 spec-decode visibility" — when that reaches a scored cell the decode axis definition changes; TensorFold is the byte-exact reference posture for DFlash2 serving ("a draft is accepted exactly when it equals the token serial decoding samples there"; drafted and serial output hashes comparable). Recommend the league manifest gains a spec-decode stance (scored cells spec-off, or a drafted/serial dual cell) BEFORE the fork's DFlash2 reaches a re-arm trigger. (4) GDN rollback lead: `gdn_capture.py` — verify-window rollback re-runs the Gated DeltaNet recurrence over only the kept prefix from the recorded initial state + keeps the matching conv tail, bit-identical to serial steps, NO re-feed — the Rapid-MLX `9c9a1bd` hybrid-recurrent-resume class (Bench 769 lane); reference implementation for the riir-train/riir-ai GDN serving lanes. **NOT transferable (each with its measured negative)**: the 27B lane kernels need M5-generation Metal tensor units (our M3 Max falls back to MLX kernels + width-checked, not bit-identical, drafts); Flash Next is 113 GB 4-bit (unloadable on 128 GB); copy-only drafting caps at 1.2-1.5x (17-35% of 225 recorded agent sessions copyable — independently CONFIRMS the Bench 760 spec-decode NO-PORT economics); the fusion-payback negatives above.
